  }, [kind, rcSections]);

  /**
   * The student's own standard clock, in minutes — 35 unless they carry extra
   * time. Every percentage on the timing control is a percentage of this, so
   * 100% means "my normal section" for everyone.
   */
  const baseMinutes =
    settings.defaultTimingMode === '52.5' ? 52.5 : settings.defaultTimingMode === '70' ? 70 : 35;

  /**
   * Load every question the student has answered and fold it into per-section
   * freshness. Only the LR runner writes attempts, so this is LR-only and the
   * control is hidden for RC rather than reporting every RC section fresh on
   * the strength of rows that were never written.
   */
  const { classId, loading: classIdLoading } = useClassId();
--- around 245-255 ---
      let mode: TimerMode;
      let customMins: number | undefined;
      if (timing.mode === 'unlimited') {
        mode = 'unlimited';
      } else if (timing.percent === 100) {
        // 100% IS the student's standing default, whatever that is, so hand
        // the timer the same mode it would have got before this control existed.
        mode = settings.defaultTimingMode;
      } else {
        // Everything else is a derived clock. customMinutes is consumed as
        // `minutes * 60 * 1000`, so a fractional value is exact to the second
        // and 94% of 35:00 runs for 32:54 rather than a rounded 33:00.
        mode = 'custom';
        customMins = (baseMinutes * timing.percent) / 100;
      }
      onStartSection({
--- accommodation labels 485-500 ---
          // and the two above it are the accommodations the LSAT actually
          // grants — +50% and +100%. The old speed-training presets (97%,
          // 94%, 70%) and the custom stepper read as arbitrary numbers on
          // a panel whose job is to start a real section.
          allowedPercents={[100, 150, 200]}
          // RC's clock is a per-passage budget with only three paces, and
          // no stopwatch to fall back to.
          allowUnlimited={kind === 'lr'}
          labels={{ 100: 'Standard', 150: 'Time and a half', 200: 'Double time' }}
          accommodationNote={kind === 'rc' ? undefined : <AccommodationNote baseMinutes={baseMinutes} />}

export function legLabel(leg: PracticeTestLeg): string {
  return leg.kind === 'lr' ? 'Logical Reasoning' : 'Reading Comprehension';
}

/** "35:00" — one leg's clock at the run's pace, or null when untimed. */
export function formatPracticeTestClock(pace: PracticeTestPaceId): string | null {
  const seconds = practiceTestPace(pace).sectionSeconds;
  if (seconds === null) return null;
  return `${Math.floor(seconds / 60)}:${String(seconds % 60).padStart(2, '0')}`;
}

=== runner: pace -> timer ===
 * drift apart.
 */
export function isSittingReturn(path: string | null | undefined): boolean {
  return !!path?.startsWith(PRACTICE_TEST_PATH_PREFIX);
}

function timerModeForPace(pace: PracticeTestPaceId): TimerMode {
  switch (pace) {
    case 'time-and-a-half':
      return '52.5';
    case 'double-time':
      return '70';
    case 'untimed':
      return 'unlimited';
    default:
      return '35';
  }
}

/**
 * Which run and which leg a runner was launched for.
 *
 * The runner needs this to key its closeout bookkeeping, and it cannot be
 * recovered from `returnTo`: that string is a routing hint, editable and
 * removable, and clock integrity must never depend on it. The leg INDEX is
 * passed rather than searched for, because `legs` can hold two legs of the same
 * section and matching by section number would pick the wrong one.
 */
export interface PtLegIdentity {
  runId: string;
  legIndex: number;
[/external_agent_tool_result]

export function createPracticeTestRun(
  blueprint: PracticeTestBlueprint,
  pace: PracticeTestPaceId,
): PracticeTestRun {
  const run: PracticeTestRun = {
    id: newRunId(),
    pt: blueprint.pt,
    pace,
    legs: blueprint.legs.map((l) => ({ ...l })),
    currentLeg: 0,
    // Two sections, then the break — the shape of the real sitting. A test that
    // somehow has fewer legs breaks after the second-to-last rather than never.
    breakAfterLeg: Math.min(2, Math.max(1, blueprint.legs.length - 1)),
    breakTaken: false,
    status: 'in-progress',
    createdAt: new Date().toISOString(),
  };
  savePracticeTestRun(run);
  return run;
}

export function savePracticeTestRun(run: PracticeTestRun): void {
  try {
    localStorage.setItem(storeKey(run.id), JSON.stringify(run));
  } catch {
    // A full or disabled store must not take the sitting down. The student
    // keeps answering; only resume-after-reload is lost.
  }

export function rcLegHref(
  run: PracticeTestRun,
  leg: PracticeTestLeg,
  legIndex: number,
): string {
  const secondsPerPassage = (() => {
    const sectionSeconds = practiceTestPace(run.pace).sectionSeconds;
    if (sectionSeconds === null) return null;
    const passages = leg.passageIds?.length || 4;
    return Math.round(sectionSeconds / passages);

    start: '1',
    returnTo: practiceTestPath(run.id),

description: Add and run accommodation pace tests
command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu
cat >> src/lib/practiceTest.test.ts <<'EOF'

/**
 * The paces are a percentage of the student's OWN standard clock, not of 35:00.
 *
 * They were absolute before: 'standard' was hard-wired to 35 minutes whatever
 * the student's standing timing mode said, so a student carrying extra time who
 * chose "Standard" was handed a 35-minute section, less time than they are
 * entitled to, on every leg of a two-hour sitting. The section door had always
 * read the setting; this door never did.
 */
describe('practice test paces', () => {
  const MIN = 60;

  it('gives a student with no accommodation the ordinary clocks', () => {
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('standard', 35 * MIN)).toBe(35 * MIN);
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('time-and-a-half', 35 * MIN)).toBe(52.5 * MIN);
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('double-time', 35 * MIN)).toBe(70 * MIN);
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('untimed', 35 * MIN)).toBeNull();
  });

  it("treats an accommodated student's own clock as Standard", () => {
    // The defect: this returned 35 minutes for a student entitled to 52.5.
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('standard', 52.5 * MIN)).toBe(52.5 * MIN);
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('standard', 70 * MIN)).toBe(70 * MIN);
  });

  it('scales the accommodations off that base rather than off 35 minutes', () => {
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('time-and-a-half', 52.5 * MIN)).toBe(78.75 * MIN);
    expect(practiceTestSectionSeconds('double-time', 52.5 * MIN)).toBe(105 * MIN);
  });

  it('labels every pace, and states the clock the student will actually get', () => {
    const paces = practiceTestPaces(52.5 * MIN);
    expect(paces.map((p) => p.id)).toEqual(['standard', 'time-and-a-half', 'double-time', 'untimed']);
    expect(paces.find((p) => p.id === 'standard')?.sectionSeconds).toBe(52.5 * MIN);
    expect(formatPracticeTestClock(practiceTestSectionSeconds('standard', 52.5 * MIN))).toBe('52:30');
    expect(formatPracticeTestClock(null)).toBeNull();
  });

  it('reads a run back at the clock it was created with, not the current setting', () => {
    const run = { pace: 'standard', sectionSeconds: 52.5 * MIN } as never;
    expect(practiceTestRunSectionSeconds(run)).toBe(52.5 * MIN);
  });

  it('falls back to the old absolute reading for runs stored before this existed', () => {
    const legacy = { pace: 'time-and-a-half' } as never;
    expect(practiceTestRunSectionSeconds(legacy)).toBe(52.5 * MIN);
  });
});
EOF

python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/pages/TestingCentre.test.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
block = '''
  /**
   * The practice-test door's paces are percentages of the student's own clock.
   * They were absolute before, so a student carrying extra time who chose
   * "Standard" was handed a 35-minute section on every leg of a two-hour
   * sitting: less time than they are entitled to, in the room that exists to
   * rehearse test day.
   */
  describe("accommodated timing", () => {
    afterEach(() => {
      settingsMock.defaultTimingMode = "35";
      Object.keys(localStorage)
        .filter((k) => k.startsWith("lsatu:practice-test:"))
        .forEach((k) => localStorage.removeItem(k));
    });

    const beginPracticeTest = async () => {
      renderPage();
      fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: /Take a practice test/ }));
      await waitFor(() => screen.getByRole("combobox", { name: /PREPTEST/i }));
      openPrepTests();
      fireEvent.click(await screen.findByRole("option", { name: /PrepTest 131/ }));
      fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: /Begin practice test/ }));
      const key = Object.keys(localStorage).find((k) => k.startsWith("lsatu:practice-test:"));
      return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(key as string) as string);
    };

    it("starts an unaccommodated sitting on the ordinary 35 minute clock", async () => {
      const run = await beginPracticeTest();
      expect(run.pace).toBe("standard");
      expect(run.sectionSeconds).toBe(35 * 60);
    });

    it("starts an accommodated sitting on the student's own clock, not on 35 minutes", async () => {
      settingsMock.defaultTimingMode = "52.5";
      const run = await beginPracticeTest();
      expect(run.pace).toBe("standard");
      expect(run.sectionSeconds).toBe(52.5 * 60);
    });

    it("states the clock the accommodated student will actually get", async () => {
      settingsMock.defaultTimingMode = "70";
      renderPage();
      fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: /Take a practice test/ }));
      await waitFor(() => screen.getByRole("combobox", { name: /PREPTEST/i }));
      // The note that tells them the standing setting is in play at all.
      expect(screen.getByText(/You have double time\\./)).toBeTruthy();
    });
  });
});'''
assert s.rstrip().endswith('});')
s = s.rstrip()
s = s[: s.rfind('});')] + block.lstrip('\n') + '\n'
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('ok')

import "@testing-library/jest-dom";

// Supabase auth client reads localStorage on init; provide a full implementation
// so it doesn't emit unhandled rejections in the jsdom test environment.
const _localStore: Record<string, string> = {};
const localStorageMock: Storage = {
  getItem:    (k) => _localStore[k] ?? null,
  setItem:    (k, v) => { _localStore[k] = String(v); },
  removeItem: (k) => { delete _localStore[k]; },
  clear:      () => { Object.keys(_localStore).forEach((k) => delete _localStore[k]); },
  get length() { return Object.keys(_localStore).length; },
  key:        (i) => Object.keys(_localStore)[i] ?? null,
};
Object.defineProperty(window, "localStorage", { value: localStorageMock, writable: true });
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit the accommodation behaviour fix
command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
A practice test runs on the student's own clock

The practice-test door's paces were absolute. 'standard' was hard-wired to 35
minutes whatever the student's standing timing mode said, so a student carrying
extra time who chose "Standard" was handed a 35-minute section, less time than
they are entitled to, on every leg of a two-hour sitting. 'time-and-a-half'
mapped to a flat 52.5 and 'double-time' to a flat 70, which for that same
student is their normal clock and one-and-a-half times it, not the
accommodations those words name.

The section door has always read the setting (SectionSelector's baseMinutes).
This door never did, so the same two words meant two different clocks in one
room, and the room is the one that exists to rehearse test day.

Paces are now a percentage of the student's own standard section: 100, 150 and
200. The resolved seconds are stored on the run at creation and read back from
there, so changing the timing setting halfway through a sitting cannot move the
clock under it, and runs stored before this existed fall back to the old
absolute reading. The timer takes 'custom' with the exact figure, which is what
carries the fractional 52.5 and 78.75 minute cases the named modes cannot
express.

The RC leg had the same defect: its per-passage budget divided an absolute
section length, so an accommodated student's passages got a share of 35 minutes.
It reads the run's own clock now.

The door also carries the AccommodationNote the section door has always had.
Without it this door offered two accommodations while saying nothing about the
standing setting those percentages are taken from.

Verified in the browser. With no accommodation: Standard 35, Time & a Half 53,
Double Time 70, Untimed no clock. On a 52.5 setting: Standard 53, Time & a Half
79, Double Time 105, and the note reads "You have time and a half."

Nine tests cover it, including the legacy-run fallback. The three that exercise
the door fail against the previous code and pass against this one. One of them
caught a bug in this change while it was being written: untimed's percent is
null, and a `??` fallback had quietly turned "no clock" into the standard one.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git log --oneline -1
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

Pushed and verified. One thing surfaced while collecting the strings that you should see first.

## Shipped: `776f69b` "A practice test runs on the student's own clock"

The defect was worse than mislabelling. `timerModeForPace` mapped `standard → '35'` absolutely, so an accommodated student who chose "Standard" was **handed a 35-minute section on every leg of a two-hour sitting** — less time than they're entitled to, in the room that exists to rehearse test day. The RC leg had the same bug: its per-passage budget divided an absolute section length, so their passages got a share of 35 minutes.

Paces are now percentages of the student's own clock (100/150/200), the resolved seconds are stored on the run so a mid-sitting settings change can't move the clock, and legacy runs fall back to the old reading. Verified in the browser: on a 52.5 setting the door now reads **Standard · 53 min, Time & a Half · 79, Double Time · 105**, where it previously read 35/53/70. 66/66 tests pass, typecheck clean, and the three door tests fail against the old code.

One of my new tests caught a bug in my own change mid-write: `untimed`'s percent is `null`, and a `??` fallback had quietly turned "no clock" into the standard clock.

## Strings you need to word

**1. This one is now factually false — `TestingCentre.tsx:228`**

> "All four passages are there and **the clock is the real 35:00**, so the reading load is unchanged."

The door above it now says 52:30 for an accommodated student. This sentence contradicts it. The fix is mechanical (the computed clock is already in scope as `clock`), but the sentence is yours. I did not touch it.

**2. The same accommodation, spelled two ways in one room**

| Where | Strings |
|---|---|
| `practiceTest.ts:36-37` | `Time & a Half`, `Double Time` |
| `SectionSelector.tsx:493` | `Time and a half`, `Double time` |

Ampersand vs "and", title case vs sentence case. Pick one.

**3. "Standard"** — `practiceTest.ts:35`, `SectionSelector.tsx:493`. It now correctly means "my normal section," but for an accommodated student the row reads "Standard · 53 min a section," which could read as *the* standard rather than *their* standard.

**4. The rounding disagrees with itself.** The pace menu says "Time & a Half · **53 min** a section" (52.5 rounded up) while the clock elsewhere reads "**52:30**". Same duration, two numbers.

**5. "blind review"** — `SectionSelector.tsx:519`. Promised in the pre-start line, defined nowhere in the product.

**6. "Status" / "Fresh · never attempted"** — `SectionSelector.tsx:362, 383`. House vocabulary on the first control a student meets.

**7. "the high seventies"** — `TestingCentre.tsx:229-230`. Vague where the rest of that paragraph is precise.

**8. "sitting"** as the unit — `Unfinished sittings` (`TestingCentre.tsx`), plus `Discard this sitting?` which **I wrote** in the Discard confirm. If that isn't your word, both should change together.

Remaining from the critique: the P2 disabled-button treatment ("Begin practice test" reads as enabled while inert) and the four P3 DESIGN.md Don'ts.

description: Write the student-data preservation memory
command: cat > "/c/Users/Joshu/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/student-work-must-be-preserved.md" <<'EOF'
---
name: student-work-must-be-preserved
description: Completed student work on LSAT U is never to be lost, modified, or broken by a change; preserving it outranks the change.
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

Work a student has already completed on LSAT U must not be lost, altered, or
rendered unreadable by anything I ship. Attempts, sessions, flags, wrong answers,
assignments, in-progress and finished practice-test sittings, RRC submissions,
scores and history are the student's record of their own effort. Preserving them
outranks whatever change is being made.

**Why:** the record is the product. A student pays for coaching and the platform
is where that work lives between sessions; PRODUCT.md's first principle is that
every number traces to real stored data. Silently dropping or rewriting a
completed sitting destroys evidence of hours of a real person's work and cannot
be regenerated. Joshua stated this directly on 2026-09-09 after a push incident,
as a rule rather than a preference.

**How to apply:**
- Changing a persisted shape (a localStorage run, a Supabase row, a stored
  config) means adding an OPTIONAL field with a fallback for records written
  before it existed. Never make an existing record unreadable. Example: adding
  `sectionSeconds` to `PracticeTestRun` on 2026-09-09 kept it optional and read
  it through `practiceTestRunSectionSeconds`, which falls back to the old
  absolute reading for runs stored earlier.
- A value already resolved for an in-flight sitting is frozen. Do not let a
  later settings change move the clock, the score basis, or the question set
  under work already underway.
- Destructive controls get a confirm, not a single click, and deletion is the
  student's choice rather than a side effect.
- Never run a migration, cleanup, backfill, or reset against student tables to
  make a change easier. Ask first, every time.
- When a refactor touches stored data, say plainly in the report what happens to
  records written before it.

Related: [[lsatu-content-lives-in-postgres]], [[ai-os-safety-rules]],
[[push-only-your-own-commits]].

## assistant

Saved to memory as a standing rule, indexed in `MEMORY.md`.

I checked today's shipped work against it, and it holds:

- **`sectionSeconds` on `PracticeTestRun`** was added as an *optional* field read through `practiceTestRunSectionSeconds`, which falls back for runs written before it existed. No stored sitting became unreadable. There's a test pinning exactly that fallback.
- **In-flight sittings are frozen.** The resolved clock is stored at creation and read back, so changing the timing setting mid-sitting can't move the clock under work already underway.
- **Discard got harder, not easier** — a confirm where there was a single click, and the deletion is still the student's choice, never a side effect.
- **No migration, backfill, cleanup or reset** was run against any student table.

One consequence you should know rather than discover: an **already in-progress** sitting created before this change carries no stored clock, so it keeps the old absolute reading. For an accommodated student that means a sitting they started earlier will still finish on 35-minute sections. That's the rule working as intended — I'd rather leave an old sitting on the clock it began with than silently re-time it halfway through — but it means the fix reaches them on their *next* sitting, not the current one. If you'd rather I repaired in-flight runs instead, that's a deliberate rewrite of stored student records and I'd want you to say so explicitly.

The one that did get sloppy today was the push, not the data. Both are now covered by memory: push by explicit SHA, and never let student work be the casualty of a change.

## Where it started
Session picked up from a handoff whose single OPEN item was "which surface was Joshua checking when he said he couldn't see the Testing Centre work?" I checked production before re-asking and found the work was already live; Joshua then said that question was a misunderstanding and to drop it. He redirected to: make the job properly done, run an `$impeccable critique`, verify against the visual page. That produced a scored critique, and the rest of the session was fixing its findings one at a time on his instruction.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **`e1b2507` Testing Centre controls get a boundary you can see** — WCAG 1.4.11 P0. The token block at `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\pages\testing-centre.css` was `html.dark`-only, so light fell through to global `--input` and all controls measured 1.25:1. Added a light sibling scoping `--input` to `--classroom-line-strong` (3.14:1). `--ring` deliberately NOT set in light: global is `0 0% 20%`, overwriting would weaken focus. Also `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\drill\SectionTimingPicker.tsx:263` `border-border` → `border-input` (was failing 1.32:1 light / 1.37:1 dark).
- **`aa6bb7e` Discard asks before it destroys a sitting** — P1. Inline two-step confirm in `ResumeList`, not a dialog (this room has no modal). Focus moves to **Keep**, never Discard. Confirm resets if the sitting leaves from under it.
- **`dc25049` Opening a door stops throwing the room off the screen** — P2. Scroll anchored on `.testing-doors` not the panel, and only fires when the panel is out of reach (`PANEL_VISIBLE_MARGIN = 120`).
- **`776f69b` A practice test runs on the student's own clock** — P2, real correctness bug. `timerModeForPace` mapped `standard → '35'` absolutely, so an accommodated student on 52.5 got 35-minute sections for a whole sitting. Paces are now percentages of their base; resolved seconds persisted on the run; RC leg had the same defect. Added `AccommodationNote` to that door.
- **`ece5480` One spelling for an accommodation, and a clock that is the student's** — the short-RC caveat's hard-coded "the real 35:00" now reads the chosen pace and drops out when untimed; unified `Time & a Half`/`Double Time` → `Time and a half`/`Double time` (chose the section door's form because `AccommodationNote` prose already uses it).
- **`fccaa82` The last copy of the old accommodation spelling** — `rcSections.ts` RC_SECTION_PACES, dormant (no callers) but would reintroduce the drift.
